```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\subsection{AI-Generated Text Detection}
5	\label{sec:related-detection}
6	
7	Methods for distinguishing human-written from machine-generated text range from statistical tests \cite{gltr,grover,release_strategies} through zero-shot approaches \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,dna_gpt} to learned classifiers \cite{ghostbuster,feat_aug_transformer,reliable_detection,ld_score}, with extensions to intrinsic dimensionality \cite{intrinsic_dimension}, surprisal diversity \cite{diveye}, and distributional gaps \cite{generator_barrier}; see \citet{wu_survey_2025} and \citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.
8	Recent work moves beyond binary decisions---recognizing collaboration roles \cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \cite{semeval2024_task8}---but all target \emph{source} or \emph{production mode} taxonomies rather than recursive generation \emph{depth}; detection scores exhibit high cross-generator variance as depth predictors (binary F1 0.52--0.71; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}) \cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.
9	Our contribution is the task formulation (generation depth classification \cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,coral_ordinal}) and the finding that combining drifting and stationary features yields consistent cross-generator performance, orthogonal to source attribution \cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.
10	
11	\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}
12	\label{sec:related-collapse}
13	
14	Self-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}; these studies address \emph{training-loop} degradation, whereas we study \emph{inference chains} with fixed parameters.
15	Iterative self-refinement \cite{self_refine} targets output improvement through feedback rather than unconstrained recursive generation.
16	Two concurrent works are most directly relevant: \citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains showing progressive information loss, and \citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics bounding distinguishable depth to approximately three classes.
17	We differ by studying \emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, framing depth as \emph{ordinal classification} (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+), and providing DDR feature-level diagnostics revealing \emph{what} converges (location) versus what remains stable (shape)---complementing their analysis of \emph{why} convergence occurs.
18	That all three works independently converge on ${\sim}$3 distinguishable classes---from Markovian dynamics \cite{markovian_chains}, information loss \cite{broken_telephone}, and feature compression (this work)---suggests a shared ceiling.
19	
20	\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}
21	\label{sec:related-diversity}
22	
23	Declining lexical diversity in LLM text is documented in training \cite{curious_decline} and deployment \cite{writing_diversity}; our surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) are classical stylometric measures \cite{stamatatos_survey,koppel_authorship} that we extend beyond generator identity \cite{uchendu_attribution} to the depth dimension under top-$p$ sampling \cite{nucleus_sampling}.
24	
25	\subsection{Data Quality and Provenance}
26	\label{sec:related-provenance}
27	
28	Scalable data curation relies on quality signals \cite{dsir,qurating,ccnet}; generation depth is a complementary provenance signal.
29	Proactive watermarking \cite{watermark_llm,synthid_text,aaronson_watermark} embeds identifiable patterns, but strength decays across multi-step chains \cite{chainwash}, motivating passive methods; our NLL analysis connects to \citet{min_k_percent} (probability-tail membership inference), extending to full distributional analysis for depth.
30	
```

</details>

Web search results for query: "markovian generation chains large language models Geng Mohamed Shang 2026"

Links: [{"title":"Guokan SHANG | Research Scientist | PhD | Mohamed bin Zayed University of Artificial Intelligence, Abu Dhabi | MBZUAI | Research profile","url":"https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Guokan-Shang"},{"title":"[2603.11228] Markovian Generation Chains in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11228"},{"title":"Markovian Generation Chains in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.11228"},{"title":"LLM as a Broken Telephone: Iterative Generation Distorts Information | Request PDF","url":"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/394300051_LLM_as_a_Broken_Telephone_Iterative_Generation_Distorts_Information"},{"title":"Markovian Generation Chains in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2603.11228"},{"title":"Markovian Generation Chains in Large Language Models | OpenReview","url":"https://openreview.net/forum?id=nTDn4OMiym"}]

I found research on this topic! Here's what the search revealed:

The paper "Markovian Generation Chains in Large Language Models" was authored by Mingmeng Geng, Amr Mohamed, Guokan Shang, Michalis Vazirgiannis, and Thierry Poibeau, and it was published in March 2026.

The paper addresses the question of how texts evolve when repeatedly processed by LLMs, defining this iterative inference process as Markovian generation chains, where each step takes a specific prompt template and the previous output as input, without including any prior memory.

In iterative rephrasing and round-trip translation experiments, the output either converges to a small recurrent set or continues to produce novel sentences over a finite horizon, with the process either increasing or reducing sentence diversity depending on factors such as the temperature parameter and the initial input sentence.

The paper is available on arXiv (paper ID 2603.11228) and also on OpenReview, where it has generated discussion in the research community.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Based on the search results, I found information about the research paper you're looking for:

The paper "LLM as a Broken Telephone: Iterative Generation Distorts Information" was authored by Amr Mohamed, Mingmeng Geng, Michalis Vazirgiannis, and Guokan Shang.

The study investigates whether LLMs similarly distort information through iterative generation like the "broken telephone" effect in chained human communication, and finds that distortion accumulates over time, influenced by language choice and chain complexity.

The researchers quantified this distortion using a combination of textual relevance metrics (BLEU, ROUGE, CHR-F, METEOR, BERTScore) and factuality assessment (FActScore).

While degradation is inevitable, it can be mitigated through strategic prompting techniques. These findings contribute to discussions on the long-term effects of AI-mediated information propagation, raising important questions about the reliability of LLM-generated content in iterative workflows.

The paper was published in the Proceedings of the 63rd Annual Meeting of the Association for Computational Linguistics (ACL) in July 2025, and the code implementation is available on GitHub.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related}\n\n\\subsection{AI-Generated Text Detection}\n\\label{sec:related-detection}\n\nMethods for distinguishing human-written from machine-generated text range from statistical tests \\cite{gltr,grover,release_strategies} through zero-shot approaches \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,dna_gpt} to learned classifiers \\cite{ghostbuster,feat_aug_transformer,reliable_detection,ld_score}, with extensions to intrinsic dimensionality \\cite{intrinsic_dimension}, surprisal diversity \\cite{diveye}, and distributional gaps \\cite{generator_barrier}; see \\citet{wu_survey_2025} and \\citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.\nRecent work moves beyond binary decisions---recognizing collaboration roles \\cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \\cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \\cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \\cite{semeval2024_task8}---but all target \\emph{source} or \\emph{production mode} taxonomies rather than recursive generation \\emph{depth}; detection scores exhibit high cross-generator variance as depth predictors (binary F1 0.52--0.71; Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}) \\cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.\nOur contribution is the task formulation (generation depth classification \\cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,coral_ordinal}) and the finding that combining drifting and stationary features yields consistent cross-generator performance, orthogonal to source attribution \\cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.\n\n\\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}\n\\label{sec:related-collapse}\n\nSelf-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \\cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \\cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}; these studies address \\emph{training-loop} degradation, whereas we study \\emph{inference chains} with fixed parameters.\nIterative self-refinement \\cite{self_refine} targets output improvement through feedback rather than unconstrained recursive generation.\nTwo concurrent works are most directly relevant: \\citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains showing progressive information loss, and \\citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics bounding distinguishable depth to approximately three classes.\nWe differ by studying \\emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, framing depth as \\emph{ordinal classification} (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+), and providing DDR feature-level diagnostics revealing \\emph{what} converges (location) versus what remains stable (shape)---complementing their analysis of \\emph{why} convergence occurs.\nThat all three works independently converge on ${\\sim}$3 distinguishable classes---from Markovian dynamics \\cite{markovian_chains}, information loss \\cite{broken_telephone}, and feature compression (this work)---suggests a shared ceiling.\n\n\\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}\n\\label{sec:related-diversity}\n\nDeclining lexical diversity in LLM text is documented in training \\cite{curious_decline} and deployment \\cite{writing_diversity}; our surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) are classical stylometric measures \\cite{stamatatos_survey,koppel_authorship} that we extend beyond generator identity \\cite{uchendu_attribution} to the depth dimension under top-$p$ sampling \\cite{nucleus_sampling}.\n\n\\subsection{Data Quality and Provenance}\n\\label{sec:related-provenance}\n\nScalable data curation relies on quality signals \\cite{dsir,qurating,ccnet}; generation depth is a complementary provenance signal.\nProactive watermarking \\cite{watermark_llm,synthid_text,aaronson_watermark} embeds identifiable patterns, but strength decays across multi-step chains \\cite{chainwash}, motivating passive methods; our NLL analysis connects to \\citet{min_k_percent} (probability-tail membership inference), extending to full distributional analysis for depth.\n",

see \\citet{wu_survey_2025} and \\citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.\nRAID \\cite{raid} provides a comprehensive binary detection benchmark spanning multiple generators and attack strategies; our work extends beyond binary to ordinal depth estimation.\nRecent work moves beyond binary decisions---recognizing collaboration roles \\cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \\cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \\cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \\cite{semeval2024_task8}---but all target \\emph{source} or \\emph{production mode} taxonomies rather than recursive generation \\emph{depth}; detection scores exhibit high cross-generator variance as depth predictors (binary F1 0.52--0.71; Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}) \\cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.\nOur contribution is the task formulation (generation depth classification \\cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,coral_ordinal}) and the finding that combining drifting and stationary features yields consistent cross-generator performance, orthogonal to source attribution \\cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.\n\n\\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}\n\\label{sec:related-collapse}\n\nSelf-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \\cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \\cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}; these studies address \\emph{training-loop} degradation, whereas we study \\emph{inference chains} with fixed parameters.\nIterative self-refinement \\cite{self_refine} targets output improvement through feedback rather than unconstrained recursive generation.\nTwo concurrent works are most directly relevant: \\citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains showing progressive information loss, and \\citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics bounding distinguishable depth to approximately three classes.\nWe differ by studying \\emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, framing depth as \\emph{ordinal classification} (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+), and providing DDR feature-level diagnostics revealing \\emph{what} converges (location) versus what remains stable (shape)---complementing their analysis of \\emph{why} convergence occurs.\nThat all three works independently converge on ${\\sim}$3 distinguishable classes---from Markovian dynamics \\cite{markovian_chains}, information loss \\cite{broken_telephone}, and feature compression (this work)---suggests a shared ceiling.\n\n\\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}\n\\label{sec:related-diversity}\n\nDeclining lexical diversity in LLM text is documented in training \\cite{curious_decline} and deployment \\cite{writing_diversity}; our surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) are classical stylometric measures \\cite{stamatatos_survey,koppel_authorship} that we extend beyond generator identity \\cite{uchendu_attribution} to the depth dimension under top-$p$ sampling \\cite{nucleus_sampling}.\n\n\\subsection{Data Quality and Provenance}\n\\label{sec:related-provenance}\n\nScalable data curation relies on quality signals \\cite{dsir,qurating,ccnet}; generation depth is a complementary provenance signal.\nProactive watermarking \\cite{watermark_llm,synthid_text,aaronson_watermark} embeds identifiable patterns, but strength decays across multi-step chains \\cite{chainwash}, motivating passive methods; our NLL analysis connects to \\citet{min_k_percent} (probability-tail membership inference), extending to full distributional analysis for depth.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\subsection{AI-Generated Text Detection}
5	\label{sec:related-detection}
6	
7	Methods for distinguishing human-written from machine-generated text range from statistical tests \cite{gltr,grover,release_strategies} through zero-shot approaches \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,dna_gpt} to learned classifiers \cite{ghostbuster,feat_aug_transformer,reliable_detection,ld_score}, with extensions to intrinsic dimensionality \cite{intrinsic_dimension}, surprisal diversity \cite{diveye}, and distributional gaps \cite{generator_barrier}; see \citet{wu_survey_2025} and \citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.
8	RAID \cite{raid} provides a comprehensive binary detection benchmark spanning multiple generators and attack strategies; our work extends beyond binary to ordinal depth estimation.
9	Recent work moves beyond binary decisions---recognizing collaboration roles \cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \cite{semeval2024_task8}, including a multi-class subtask (Task~8C) targeting generator-level source classification---but all target \emph{source} or \emph{production mode} taxonomies rather than recursive generation \emph{depth}; detection scores exhibit high cross-generator variance as depth predictors (binary F1 0.52--0.71; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}) \cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.
10	Our contribution is the task formulation (generation depth classification \cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,coral_ordinal}) and the finding that combining drifting and stationary features yields consistent cross-generator performance, orthogonal to source attribution \cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.
11	
12	\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}
13	\label{sec:related-collapse}
14	
15	Self-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}; these studies address \emph{training-loop} degradation, whereas we study \emph{inference chains} with fixed parameters.
16	Notably, \citet{strong_model_collapse} predict progressive tail coverage loss under iterative retraining; our DDR analysis reveals an orthogonal pattern in inference chains---location parameters drift while distributional shape is preserved---suggesting that fixed-parameter generation imposes different constraints than recursive training.
17	Iterative self-refinement \cite{self_refine} targets output improvement through feedback rather than unconstrained recursive generation.
18	Two concurrent works are most directly relevant: \citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains showing progressive information loss, and \citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics bounding distinguishable depth to approximately three classes.
19	We differ by studying \emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, framing depth as \emph{ordinal classification} (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+), and providing DDR feature-level diagnostics revealing \emph{what} converges (location) versus what remains stable (shape)---complementing their analysis of \emph{why} convergence occurs.
20	That all three works independently converge on ${\sim}$3 distinguishable classes---from Markovian dynamics \cite{markovian_chains}, information loss \cite{broken_telephone}, and feature compression (this work)---suggests a shared ceiling.
21	
22	\begin{table}[h]
23	\centering
24	\footnotesize
25	\caption{Comparison with concurrent work on iterative generation chains.}
26	\label{tab:concurrent-comparison}
27	\begin{tabular}{lll}
28	\toprule
29	\textbf{Work} & \textbf{Setting} & \textbf{Key Distinction from Ours} \\
30	\midrule
31	\citet{broken_telephone} & Paraphrase + translation chains & Semantic distortion; relevance-based metrics \\
32	\citet{markovian_chains} & Attractor dynamics in gen.\ chains & Theoretical framework; no DDR decomposition \\
33	\citet{min_k_percent} & Membership inference (prob.-tail) & Binary detection; no depth estimation \\
34	\citet{strong_model_collapse} & Model collapse framework & Retraining regime; tail coverage loss \\
35	\midrule
36	\textbf{This work} & Non-paraphrastic continuation chains & DDR decomposition + ordinal depth + LOGO \\
37	\bottomrule
38	\end{tabular}
39	\end{table}
40	
41	\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}
42	\label{sec:related-diversity}
43	
44	Declining lexical diversity in LLM text is documented in training \cite{curious_decline} and deployment \cite{writing_diversity}; our surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) are classical stylometric measures \cite{stamatatos_survey,koppel_authorship} that we extend beyond generator identity \cite{uchendu_attribution} to the depth dimension under top-$p$ sampling \cite{nucleus_sampling}.
45	
46	\subsection{Data Quality and Provenance}
47	\label{sec:related-provenance}
48	
49	Scalable data curation relies on quality signals \cite{dsir,qurating,ccnet}; generation depth is a complementary provenance signal.
50	Proactive watermarking \cite{watermark_llm,synthid_text,aaronson_watermark} embeds identifiable patterns, but strength decays across multi-step chains \cite{chainwash}, motivating passive methods; our NLL analysis connects to \citet{min_k_percent} (probability-tail membership inference), extending to full distributional analysis for depth.
51	
```
